Original PU mapping and aliases are deliberately preserved; unmapped entries include Force India/Renault/Toro Rosso in 2018, Alfa/Racing Point/Renault/Toro Rosso in 2019, Alfa/AlphaTauri/Racing Point/Renault in 2020, and Alfa/AlphaTauri in 2021–22. Train-only imputation handles their missing PU feature. This is a fidelity limitation, not a validated physical engine assignment or proof that improved PU metadata has no value.

**Verification:** implementation checkpoint `d6e9c96` preceded scoring. Shared simulator and reporting defaults preserve the seven-feature E192 output exactly on a saved event; its existing artifact tests still pass and files were not overwritten. Original-source equivalence, target/future status invariance, earlier-source responsiveness, full target-label/grid/qualifying/pace invariance and all standard leak/pre-weekend/profile/guard/metric checks pass. Independent saved-ranking audit reproduces every winner/podium metric for E193; complete artifact audit verifies simulated count normalization, unchanged frozen reference scores, winning-team hits, pseudocount probabilities and log-loss. No new source, outside text, market input, production change or 2026 evaluation. Saved `results/e193_inner_*`; goal active and unmet.

**Next research audit:** distinguish forecasting the *mean* pace from forecasting its *uncertainty*. E18/E75 use fixed global noise; inspect E57, E74–E80 and prior confidence/persistence work before proposing any different variance model. Only a genuinely untested, pre-registered prequential error-variance measurement can justify a new probabilistic simulator; no noise sweep or relabelled failed selector. No new model is registered by this note.

### E194 — Is prequential relative-pace error scale predictable by team? (registered before scoring)

**Prior-work audit:** E57 tested podium confidence/abstention, E133–E134 tested historical *grid* error volatility, E75 fixed shared Monte Carlo noise, and E80's Kalman covariance follows fixed process/observation variances rather than measured surprise amplitudes. Those do not establish whether earlier one-step **relative race-pace errors** predict later error scale for contenders. Test that premise before any heteroscedastic simulator. This is not another sigma sweep after E193's failed fixed setting.

**Frozen mean source:** E192/E193 ridge alpha500, median/scaler fit on strictly earlier 2019+ observed pace labels, half-life100 and the same base pre-weekend features. Create chronological mean forecasts from 2019 R6 onward (require at least five earlier 2019+ events), using 2018 only as historical feature context. Evaluate all 61 inner races 2020–22. After each event, for ≥5 drivers with observed canonical pace, compute error `(actual gap−forecast mean) − mean(actual−forecast over observed entrants)`. This removes a common shift irrelevant to race order. Missing labels do not create zero errors. Only then reveal team-race mean squared relative errors, averaging available cars. No target pace/error is a prediction feature; no 2026/outer source or score.

**One scale predictor:** exponential history half-life20 events, no era reset or lineage tuning. Global scale = weighted mean of strictly earlier team-race MSE observations; team scale shrinks its own weighted MSE toward that global value by **eight pseudo team-races** (inherited from the earlier grid-volatility design, not tuned here). No-history team→global; no error history anywhere→fixed 1 percentage-point²; floor both at 1e−4. For a matched null, permute observed team-race MSEs among observed teams within each completed event (seed194), preserving source availability and global distribution. Predict all entrants, independent of current measurements.

**Diagnostic targets/gate:** primary = six entrants selected by the unchanged pre-weekend race-points/form selector; full field secondary. This is an error-scale diagnostic, not selective winner accuracy. Report all 61 race forecasts and exact actual-label coverage. Compare Gaussian negative log score `.5*(log(scale)+error²/scale)` (constant omitted), MSE as a prediction of error², and correlation(team scale,error²), with race-cluster bootstrap intervals and the matched null. Require ≥50 races with labels for ≥5/6 preselected contenders; primary mean log-score gain ≥.01 nats with 95% lower bound >0, positive primary squared-error-scale MSE gain, primary correlation ≥.15, and nonnegative full-field mean log-score gain. Otherwise close this predictor without adjusting shrinkage, decay, centering or contender selection. Passing only permits a separately registered winner-simulation test; it is not a winner-accuracy claim.

**Verification:** hand-computed shrinkage/fallback, field-shift invariance and ≥5-label rule; target/future source-error mutations leave target scale predictions exact, earlier edits affect them. Mean forecasts invariant to current labels/grid/quali. Fit guards and standard leak/metric checks. Independent scalar source summation and saved coverage/score/null/bootstrap reconstruction. Checkpoint before measurement. No new external input, account, API call, market data or production change; objective remains 75% exact pre-weekend winners over all races.

**Result (E194), error-scale diagnostic only:** all **61** inner races have pre-weekend mean/scale forecasts (**1220 entrants**). Actual relative-pace labels exist for **1134** entrants; **340/366** preselected contender labels are observed, with **58/61** races meeting the ≥5/6 coverage threshold. No missing pace was converted to a zero error and no winner-accuracy denominator was changed.

Primary contender Gaussian pace-error log scores (constant omitted; lower better): global **0.949793**, team-specific **1.071002**, identity-shuffle null **1.002816**. Global-minus-team score gain **-0.121209 [-0.441616,+0.050125]** nats, failing the positive-gain gate. Correlation(team scale, future squared relative error) **-0.053907 [-0.103953,+0.060136]**, below +.15. Error-scale prediction MSE gain **-0.071471** (percentage-point⁴ units), and full-field log-score gain **-0.170229**: both negative. The interval does not establish that the team predictor is always harmful; it provides no evidence of the required improvement.

**Verdict: registered measurement gate failed; no heterogeneous-noise simulator or winner feature/model.** Do not retune decay, shrinkage, centering, pooling or contender selection after these outcomes. This tests one specific past-team error-scale predictor and is not a proof that all uncertainty is unpredictable. No race-winner accuracy was measured here; the 75% all-race pre-weekend objective remains unmet and active.

**Verification:** checkpoint `0e1fc44` preceded scoring. Synthetic shrinkage/fallback/floor, common-shift invariance, five-observed-driver label threshold and target-mean invariance pass. Standard leak/pre-weekend/profile/guard/metric checks pass. Independent artifact audit reconstructs raw relative errors, team-race source MSEs, every global/team/null scale by scalar historical summation, all score calculations and frozen prior-points top-six selection. It reproduces confidence intervals by a separate race-weight sufficient-statistic bootstrap. Real current/future raw-pace label changes leave target scales/predictions exact; an earlier source edit changes them. Saved `results/e194_*` retain warmup forecasts from 2019 R6 and every inner entrant, including unlabelled cases. No outside source, account, API call, market input, production change or 2026 score. Original main checkout untouched.

Before another fit, audit whether a **signed mean-forecast residual correction** is genuinely absent from E13/E21/E80 and the other form models, rather than relabeling the failed variance predictor. If duplicated, record that and seek a distinct source/mechanism; do not rerun or change the target to obtain a passing result. No further simulator is justified by E194.

### E195 — Prequential team mean-bias calibration (registered before scoring)

**Audit/hypothesis:** E13/E80 smooth raw team pace levels and teammate offsets; E21 tests teammate outcomes and longer training history. They do not explicitly calibrate a fixed global ridge forecaster using its own strictly out-of-sample, field-centered signed errors. This is a bounded calibration check using existing information, not a new physical data source or a rejection of those prior results. E194's failed squared-error predictor does not determine whether signed bias persists; no variance/simulator parameter will be changed.

**Frozen source:** use E194's saved chronological ridge means and observed relative errors, 2019 R6–2022, without refitting or selecting a mean model. Target observations are team-race means of available signed relative errors, revealed after each event; no missing label→zero conversion. One correction only: strictly earlier team observations, 20-event half-life, eight pseudo team-races at zero, no era/lineage adjustment. No history→0. Before scoring, subtract the correction's mean across **all current entrants**, not the post-race observed-pace subset. Corrected forecast = frozen mean + centered correction. Null permutes observed signed team-error means among observed teams within each completed source race (seed195), preserving source availability. No raw identifier fitting, new learner, or noise sweep.

**Measurement gate:** all 61 inner 2020–22 race forecasts retained. Primary error diagnostic = E194's fixed pre-weekend top-six contender selection and observed-label coverage; full field secondary. Require ≥50 races with ≥5/6 contender labels, primary relative error-MSE reduction ≥2% with paired race-cluster 95% MSE-gain lower bound >0, primary correction/error Pearson ≥.15, nonnegative full-field MSE gain, and positive paired mean per-race pace-order Spearman gain with 95% lower bound >0 (≥5 observed cars/race). Otherwise close this correction without retuning decay, shrinkage, centering or cohort. Passing only permits a separately registered full-denominator winner test, not an accuracy claim. Report matched null and every failed criterion; do not select the corrected model based on outer scores.

**Verification:** zero-history/shrinkage and known-sign example, centering over entered drivers including missing-label entrants, target/future raw-error mutation leaves target correction exact, earlier edits affect it. Independent scalar history, signed-label/source reconstruction, score/coverage/null and race-bootstrap audit. Standard guards/metrics; no all-season model imports, new API/account, outside text/market inputs, production change or 2026 evaluation. Checkpoint before scoring; prior mean-source files stay immutable. The goal remains 75% exact pre-weekend winners across all races.

**Result (E195), calibration diagnostic only:** retained all **61 inner races / 1,220 entrant forecasts**. Relative-pace labels cover **1,134** entrants and **340/366** preselected contenders; **58** races have ≥5/6 contender labels. Primary MSE: frozen **1.138316**, corrected **1.143312**, identity-shuffle null **1.136860** (percentage-point² units). Base-minus-corrected gain **−0.004996 [−0.028351,+0.019075]**, a **−0.4389% relative reduction** (i.e. MSE increases 0.44%). Correction/error correlation **0.031843 [−0.077850,+0.106057]**. Full-field MSE gain **−0.006689**. Paired per-race pace Spearman gain **+0.001146 [−0.007808,+0.009942]** over **60** races with ≥5 observed drivers.

**Verdict: registered gate failed.** Coverage passes; primary ≥2% MSE improvement, positive MSE-gain lower bound, ≥.15 correlation, nonnegative full-field gain and positive rank-gain lower bound all fail. A slightly positive mean ranking change without the required interval does not justify a winner model. No outer run, new 2026 scoring, promotion or post-result retuning. No exact-winner accuracy was measured by E195. The 75% all-race pre-weekend winner goal remains active and unmet; this closes only the fixed signed team-error correction, not all possible calibration methods.

**Verification:** checkpoint `ec7d5e2` was committed and pushed before scoring. Synthetic known-sign/shrinkage/no-history, all-entry centering (including entrants without current pace labels), deterministic null and future-source cutoff pass. Standard leak/pre-weekend/profile/guard/metric checks pass. Independent scalar historical summation reconstructs team corrections and shuffled null; source signed errors are rebuilt from raw saved pace and means. Frozen E194 means, labels, entrant cohort and contender flags are unchanged exactly. Independent rank-transform correlations and race-weight bootstrap reproduce scores, confidence intervals and every gate criterion. Scrambling current/future raw pace leaves current and earlier corrections exact; an earlier signed-error change affects target corrections. E194 source artifacts and original main checkout remain unchanged. Saved `results/e195_*` include warmup and unlabelled entrant rows; no outside source, API, account, market or production change.

**Next step:** reconcile the remaining structured-data backlog against completed E101–E195 before registering another experiment. E194/E195 provide no justification for further team-error decay/shrinkage, variance or bias sweeps. A follow-up needs a distinct admissible measurement or mechanism, with a source-coverage and timing audit; do not rename these failed corrections or substitute diagnostic gains for exact-winner accuracy.

### E196 — Historical minute-weather source recovery (registered before acquisition)

**Decision audit:** A1/A2/A3/A5/A6 and passing/strategy refinements already have failed measurement or forecast gates. E194/E195 do not license another error-history sweep. Two distinct remaining checks are physical supplier-metadata coverage (known legacy PU-map caveats) and B9's matched thermal/wind response. Choose B9 source recovery first: E123 established only that weather was absent from the local cache, explicitly saying no weather feed was newly acquired. FastF1 ingestion uses `weather=False`; absence does not establish archive unavailability. Thermal response is an unverified hypothesis, not an accuracy claim. Current FastF1 official source/docs confirm the public `Session.load(weather=True)` flag and minute weather timestamps; installed version 3.9.0 source inspected. No outside text is a model input.

**Fixed acquisition pilot:** the same three preselected 2019 races as E190 (rounds 5 Spain, 12 Hungary, 14 Italy). Read their existing cached laps without network, then permit only that session's `WeatherData.jsonStream` on the standard F1 live-timing host or FastF1's built-in mirror. Block every other outgoing HTTP request, including authentication/account endpoints; disable the installed adapter's token lookup so it cannot read credentials before sending. No credential file, login, purchase or new account. Use public `Session.load(laps=False,telemetry=False,weather=True,messages=False)`; preserve failed/missing feeds as unavailable, never zero-fill weather. Stop this access route if protected/unavailable; no circumventing authentication. No outer/2026 feed or outcome.

**Contract:** input = cached prior-session laps plus minute weather samples keyed by session-relative time; output = clean-lap rows with the most recent weather sample at or before lap start, within 120 seconds. Unknown/out-of-range samples remain missing. Reuse E190's unchanged green/no-pit/accurate/lap-time clean filter; do not interpret temperature/time associations as causal tyre effects. No forecast feature or fitted pace response. Save all weather channels and aligned clean laps, acquisition/match coverage and per-race temperature range. Empty/malformed feeds fail the source gate and retain an error type.

**Feasibility gate:** all three sessions must have ≥100 distinct valid minute timestamps, ≥95% finite TrackTemp samples, and ≥90% clean-lap matches. At least two sessions must have matched TrackTemp p95−p05 ≥3°C and ≥10 drivers with ≥20 matched dry laps each. These thresholds authorize only a separately registered, confound-controlled repeatability/temporal-transfer measurement; not a winner model. Do not replace circuits or relax thresholds after looking. Earlier qualifying and grid are not used, and no target-event weather forecast or actual weather enters a prediction.

**Verification:** synthetic backward-only matching, exact tolerance boundary, missing/empty source, duplicate timestamps rejection and future-sample invariance; restricted-network allowlist tests; independently reconstruct saved match timestamps/age and coverage/range/gate. Standard leakage/guard/metric suite. Commit/push setup before acquisition and results separately. Primary goal remains 75% exact pre-weekend winner accuracy over all races; prior turn E195 was progress (completed evidence closed a candidate), not a wait or success.

**Result (E196): public-access source gate failed, no weather measurement or winner model.** Cached lap loading succeeds for all three fixed 2019 sessions. No session loads weather: the primary timing host returns **HTTP 401** and the package's built-in mirror returns **HTTP 404** for each exact pilot WeatherData endpoint. Thus weather/lap-match/range coverage is unavailable, not zero temperature or evidence of absent thermal effects. The initial run exposed only FastF1's suppressed `DataNotLoadedError`; a diagnostic-only change records allowlisted HTTP status/error types, and one repeat of the same three endpoints established the access failure. No circuits, endpoints, thresholds or data sources were substituted. Requests for unrelated Jolpica result refreshes were blocked by the allowlist and FastF1 fell back to existing cached rows; these are not forecasting labels or new acquisitions.

**Verification and scope:** setup `d6c5f69` was committed/pushed before acquisition. Synthetic backward-only matching, 120-second boundary, missing/empty source, duplicate rejection, future-sample invariance and network allowlist tests pass, as do standard guards/leak/metric checks. Recorded result independently verifies exactly three registered sessions, primary 401/mirror 404 pairs and no successful weather samples; the saved summary has no matched-row/temperature claims. Adapter token lookup was explicitly disabled, preventing credential reads/login; account endpoints and all non-weather network requests were forbidden. Stop this unauthenticated access route as registered. Existing account-authorized ingestion, if separately considered, must be audited/registered explicitly; no account, licence or permission change was made here. No outer/2026 feed, accuracy score, pace model or production change. Goal remains active and unmet.

### E197 — Existing-account historical weather source gate (registered before authentication/acquisition)

**Why distinct from E196:** E196's primary endpoint returned 401 for all three pilot feeds; the unauthenticated route is closed. The project already ingests FastF1 using an existing F1 account and `scripts_run_ff1.sh` (documented in README and the user's handoff). That helper loads only `F1_USERNAME`/`F1_PASSWORD` at runtime without printing them, and already supports `F1_MODULE`. Reuse that authorized account mechanism, not a new account, subscription, purchase or licence. No manual credential/token inspection, display or data export. Native FastF1 3.9 password authentication and installed token/transport code audited; preserve ordinary provider access controls. Stop on auth/subscription failure, with only the exception class recorded; do not seek a different login service or expose auth bodies/headers.

**Frozen pilot/gate:** exactly E196's 2019 rounds 5,12,14, same cached clean laps, backward 120-second weather match, all coverage/range thresholds and outputs, using separate `e197_*` files. Require existing environment credentials supplied by the project helper (no interactive prompt), obtain/reuse the standard account token before the weather-only HTTP allowlist, then use the unmodified authenticated adapter for the three historical feeds. Authentication may use the package's normal identity-provider discovery/login endpoints; acquisition still forbids every non-pilot-weather network request. Do not acquire an outer/2026 feed. No feature, thermal-response fit, winner score or new forecast-weather source. Gate failure closes this acquisition attempt; a pass only permits a separately registered confound-controlled measurement test. Do not alter thresholds based on E196, whose no-sample result supplied no temperature/range outcome.

**Implementation/verification:** extend only E196's reporting prefix and choice of token-lookup context, preserving its unauthenticated default; wrapper handles missing credentials/auth errors without printing sensitive exception messages. Synthetic tests verify failure stops acquisition, success dispatches the fixed authenticated pilot, and existing weather/time/network invariants remain. Independently audit returned source samples, timestamp alignment/coverage/gate if available; preserve missing feeds if not. Commit/push setup first, results separately. E196 was progress (concrete access evidence), not a model failure or verified wait; 75% exact winners remains active and unmet.

**Result (E197): source-feasibility gate passed.** The existing account obtained primary-host **HTTP 200** for each of the three fixed 2019 feeds; no mirror or circuit substitution. Saved **373 minute samples** and **3,151 clean laps**, with **100%** backward weather matches, finite track temperature and dry coverage. Per race: Spain **132 samples / 1,062 laps**, track-temperature p95−p05 **2.7°C**; Hungary **130 / 1,268**, **6.2°C**; Italy **111 / 821**, **3.4°C**. All three have **20 drivers** with ≥20 matched dry laps; Hungary/Italy satisfy the fixed ≥3°C variation condition, so the two-of-three requirement passes. This establishes accessible measured thermal variation, not repeatable car sensitivity, a forecast weather source, causal aerodynamics/tyre behaviour or better winner accuracy.

**Verification:** setup `3af33af` was committed/pushed before authentication or source acquisition. Existing two-key helper supplied runtime credentials; no credential/token value or auth response was displayed/exported/staged, and no account/subscription/licence/purchase was created. Native password method was verified against installed 3.9.0 code: Context7's authentication excerpt still describes the older browser/addon route, so it was not treated as proof of native support. Saved acquisition audit contains only historical weather URLs/statuses; non-weather result-refresh attempts were blocked and used cached data. E196 artifacts remain unchanged. Auth-failure tests stop acquisition and sanitize exception text; source matching/network tests and all standard leak/pre-weekend/profile/guard/metric checks pass.

**Adversarial source audit:** assume the recovery is a wrong-session or stale-cache match. An independent artifact test checks the exact three registered sessions, primary HTTP200 provenance, unique timestamp/lap keys, and independently rebuilds all clean-lap sets from cache with HTTP forbidden. Binary search independently reconstructs every latest-at-or-before timestamp and matched temperature/rain flag; raw clean-lap times, coverage, quantiles and the complete gate reproduce. Original main tracked files remain unchanged. Historical weather now resides in the shared ignored cache; a future cache-only/unauthenticated read of these acquired files is not evidence of public endpoint access and must retain this account-acquisition provenance.

**Verdict/next:** B9 is reopened for a separately registered measurement, not a winner feature. Before broad extraction or response fitting, test whether temperature supplies identifiable variation beyond race progression, tyre age/compound and traffic; nearly monotone temperature can merely reproduce fuel/stint trends. No thermal-response coefficient or winner score has been inspected. Do not feed actual target weather or a post-cutoff forecast into a pre-weekend model. No new outer/2026 data/evaluation or production change. The 75% all-race exact-winner objective stays active and unmet; source success does not change the reported 48.6% exploratory outer maximum or establish its superiority over baseline.

### E198 — Does thermal variation survive fixed race/stint controls? (registered before measurement)

**Question/decision:** E197 verified an accessible source (373 minute samples, 3,151 clean laps); independent car-temperature response remains assumed, not measured. Competing explanations: temperature contributes changes not explained by race progression/stint state, or the raw range mainly duplicates fuel/time/tyre trends. A design-only support test distinguishes these before fitting any lap-time response or expanding acquisition. This is a necessary-support screen, not proof of causal identifiability or predictive usefulness. Prior turn E197 was progress: concrete source acquisition and validation.

**Frozen source/selection:** only immutable E197 aligned clean laps from 2019 rounds 5,12,14. Retain dry rows with finite TrackTemp, session-relative lap start, TyreLife and Position, nonmissing Stint/Compound and boolean close indicator. Report every excluded row and keep all three events in the denominator. No lap-duration, outcome, team performance or winner label is used in this test. No network or outer/2026 source. Split by alternating **distinct weather timestamp blocks** within each event, shared across drivers, not by individual laps that may share the same weather reading. Use full rows and both disjoint weather-block halves separately.

**One nuisance design per driver/event/subset:** intercept; normalized event elapsed time to powers1,2,3 (event min/max from all eligible source rows); stint intercept dummies (first omitted); tyre age/10 and its square interacted with each observed compound; Position/20 and close indicator. Project TrackTemp onto that fixed nuisance column span using least squares, rcond1e−10, allowing redundant/zero columns. Save actual rank and residual degrees of freedom. Do not change the polynomial order, age basis, split or controls after results. A driver/subset has usable thermal support iff ≥15 rows, ≥10 distinct weather readings, ≥10 residual degrees of freedom, residual temperature RMS ≥0.5°C and residual/raw centered temperature variance ≥0.10 (constant raw temperature→0). These are predeclared pilot-screen thresholds, not universal physical effect or sensor-precision bounds.

**Gate/tripwire:** a driver passes only if the full sample and both weather-block halves meet every threshold. Require ≥10 passing drivers in **at least two of the three fixed races** before any response fit or broader acquisition. Failure closes this within-race thermal formulation on the pilot without weakening controls or hunting another split. Passing only permits a separately registered response repeatability/temporal-transfer measurement, not a winner score/model. Minute samples within a race are correlated; row counts are not independent effective sample sizes, and the variance screen gives no confidence interval or causal claim.

**Contract/verification:** inputs are fixed pilot weather/design metadata; outputs are per-driver full/half support diagnostics and projected-temperature residuals, with no fitted lap-time coefficient. Empty/constant/rank-deficient subsets fail support explicitly; duplicate driver-lap keys or non-pilot seasons/events reject input. Synthetic confounded trend, nuisance-orthogonal variation, degenerate/duplicate source and lap-duration-label mutation checks. Independent artifact reconstruction by an orthonormal SVD projection, fixed raw selection/split/design and exact gate audit, including projection orthogonality. Use existing NumPy/pandas; current NumPy least-squares rank/rcond docs consulted. Standard guards remain; commit/push setup before measuring, results separately. No winner/podium accuracy is measured, so no paired winner bootstrap or winner-label null is implied; reported baseline winner results are unchanged. Goal stays 75% exact pre-weekend winners over all races.

**Result (E198): registered thermal-support gate failed.** All **3,151** source rows are eligible; none excluded. All **60 driver/event combinations** retain full/half diagnostics (180 rows). Passing all full/half criteria: Spain **0/20**, Hungary **15/20**, Italy **0/20**; only **one** event has ≥10 supported drivers, versus the required two. Per-subset support counts full/half0/half1: Spain **0/0/0**, Hungary **19/16/16**, Italy **0/0/0**.

Median full/half0/half1 residual temperature RMS (°C): Spain **0.1564 / 0.1417 / 0.1391**, Hungary **0.8048 / 0.7757 / 0.7353**, Italy **0.4064 / 0.3453 / 0.3200**. Median fractions of raw centered thermal variance remaining: Spain **0.0335 / 0.0279 / 0.0268**, Hungary **0.1903 / 0.1696 / 0.1590**, Italy **0.1542 / 0.1166 / 0.0885**. Every Spain and Italy driver/subset is below the fixed 0.5°C RMS screen; all Spain subsets also fall below 10% variance remaining. Italy's fractional variance is sometimes substantial but its amplitude still fails the predeclared criterion. Some short Italy half-samples also fail row/degree-of-freedom criteria. These are diagnostic descriptions, not physical sensor bounds or proof of absent temperature sensitivity.

**Verdict:** no lap-time response fit or broad acquisition under this registered formulation. Do not lower the thermal amplitude threshold, remove the time/tyre controls, change halves or select Hungary alone to obtain a pass. The historical weather asset remains valid; this negative result closes the fixed pilot support rule, not every between-event weather hypothesis, wind mechanism or causal tyre effect. No exact-winner/podium accuracy was scored, no baseline altered, no outer/2026 feed or score, and no production feature. Source recovery was not a forecast gain; the 75% exact-winner objective remains active and unmet.

**Verification:** setup `e3a84f3` was committed/pushed before measurement. Tests cover a trend entirely in the nuisance span, a known orthogonal thermal component, constant/empty/one-row subsets, all-ineligible events retained as failures, shared weather-block halves, duplicate/non-pilot input rejection and lap-duration/outcome-label invariance. Standard leak/pre-weekend/profile/guard/metric checks pass. Assuming the reported support is wrong, an independent artifact audit rebuilds selection, shared blocks, every nuisance column and all 180 ranks/degrees of freedom. It uses orthonormal SVD projection rather than fitted coefficients, verifies projection orthogonality, reproduces every residual/diagnostic and the full gate, and confirms that corrupted lap durations/outcomes change nothing. E197 source files and main tracked files remain unchanged. Saved `results/e198_*`; no requests/authentication/credentials involved.

**Next research constraint:** seek a distinct measurable mechanism; a simple thermal-slope or reduced-controls variant is not justified by this failure. Audit candidate information novelty against the full completed log before another fit. Do not treat still-accessible wind/weather channels as permission to run an unregistered parameter search or add actual target-weekend weather.

### E199 — Cached timing-stream leader-pressure source gate (registered before extraction)

**Audit/choice:** E185's nine scalar pace definitions, E179 laps led, E186 win conversion and E191 following-car traffic response do not measure whether the leader manages its pace differently with a close pursuer. That mechanism could bias pace estimates of winning cars, but remains assumed. A second candidate, legacy PU metadata, has known missing aliases and unverified physical assignments (E193); do not invent supplier identities from memory. Choose leader pressure for a bounded source test. During source-code tracing, the initial uncommitted full-lap-lag gap draft was discarded: FastF1's already-cached `_extended_timing_data` also retains a position/gap stream, enabling a more direct source check. No draft extraction, count or outcome was run.

**Fixed source/contract:** all 21 official 2019 events; cached laps and the same session's cached parsed timing stream only. Explicit HTTP prohibition and offline mode, no new authentication/credentials/acquisition, outer/2026 data, outside text or winner labels. Installed FastF1 3.9.0 parser/cache implementation audited (experimental private API dependency, not production). Convert stream driver numbers using that session's existing lap driver-number/code mapping, requiring one-to-one identity. Source rows are session timestamps with Position and GapToLeader. Keep the last parsed row per driver/timestamp, preserving original row order; report duplicates/missing values. Parse only finite numeric seconds with optional plus sign; lap-count strings and other formats stay unknown, never zero-filled.

**Causal exposure boundary, retrospective sample selection:** E190's unchanged clean green/no-pit/accurate/lap-time filter selects response laps with completed Position1, lap≥4. Those post-race comparability labels are not a within-race predictor. For each such fixed target lap, use only each driver's latest stream row at/before LapStartTime, require unique known P1/P2 and matching leader identity. P2 gap must be >0 and ≤30 seconds. Because the parser forward-fills values, define conservative gap observation time as the last **numeric value change** (or first finite value) for that driver, not the last unrelated position/interval update. Require this change time within **30 seconds before** the leader's lap start; unchanged true updates may be lost, but cannot be falsely refreshed. Record leader/chaser identities, state timestamps, conservative gap age, pressure≤2s / comfortable≥5s / intermediate. Missing/stale/conflicting snapshots stay unavailable. No subsequent gap, crossing or lap-time response is used.

**Fixed support gate:** retain all 21 events as denominator. Require ≥80% fresh snapshot coverage among clean leader response laps in ≥15 events; ≥20 leader driver-stints with at least five pressured and five comfortable observations with one common chaser and known compound; these contrasting blocks must span ≥8 races, and ≥3 teams must each have ≥3 contrasting driver-stints. Collapse multiple qualifying chasers/compounds within one driver-stint to one support count. This is a sample-support screen, not a power proof or evidence of intent. Failure closes this timing-stream formulation without changing stale tolerance, gaps or count thresholds. A pass permits only a separately registered comparable-lap response repeatability/transfer test. No pressure coefficient, pace correction, model or winner score at this gate.

### E200 — Before-lap interval-to-ahead versus crossing proxies (registered before extraction)

### E201 — Native categorical identities in the fixed winner GBM (registered before scoring)

**Novelty/decision:** a full-source search found no CatBoost/native-categorical/one-hot identity winner test. No grid/parameter/feature-subset search, ID cross-products, alternative lineage map, tuning or early stopping. Each categorical vocabulary is fit from that race's strictly earlier2019+ training rows only; unseen target categories→missing, never a combined/future vocabulary. No outer/2026 scoring, new source, market, production change or promotion at this stage.

### E202 — DRS-state novelty and raw-source audit (no outcome scoring)

**Decision question:** can a many-lap, comparable-state DRS-open versus closed measurement supply a distinct pre-weekend car characteristic for the 75% exact-winner objective? Candidate A is a reproducible state-dependent acceleration effect; candidate B is selection by traffic, fuel/programme and straight position. E9 already measures full-throttle **closed-state** 200–280 km/h acceleration; E97 measures energy/acceleration signatures; E126 tested mixed-state race SpeedST; E127 onward mixed-state position-aligned profiles. None of the inspected implementations separates comparable many-lap open/closed states. That is methodological novelty, not evidence of useful transfer. Do not reopen the failed scalar speed-trap or traffic-response formulations under a new name.

**Verified source inventory, 2026-11-17:** `data/fastf1_cache` contains **196 `car_data.ff1pkl` files, all Qualifying**, including **21 in 2019** and **zero Race**. Per-season qualifying counts 2018–26: 21,21,17,22,22,22,24,24,23. This counts filenames only, not valid samples, raw HTTP cache entries, or all possible remote sources. No cache payload was loaded, particularly no 2026 payload/outcome. Historical race lap/timing availability from E190–E200 does not imply cached race raw-car telemetry. Other sources such as OpenF1 are not covered by this inventory.

**State semantics caveat:** installed FastF1 **3.9.0** `_api.car_data` documents uncertain meanings for several DRS codes; it describes 10/12/14 as on and 8 as eligibility. More consequentially, its parser uses `Channels.get("45", 0)` and explicitly comments that DRS is no longer included in 2026. Therefore **a parser-produced zero alone cannot distinguish a genuinely recorded off state from an absent channel**. The project engine/energy masks use DRS<10, so their mask alone does not establish observed wing state when this fallback occurs. This is a source-observability warning, not a claim that any particular cached 2026 sample is absent or a newly measured predictive failure. No historical feature/score is changed or declared timing-invalid by this audit. Method documentation: [official FastF1 source](https://github.com/theOehrly/Fast-F1/blob/master/fastf1/_api.py); Context7 also recommends original car samples rather than merged/interpolated telemetry for calculations. The docs HTML endpoint returned403; installed code is the direct authority inspected here.

**Verdict:** defer the DRS-effect fit rather than acquire a historical regime-specific dataset before defining an observable deployment counterpart. No registered measurement gate was run or failed; no claim that historical DRS information is useless or intrinsically unavailable. Existing account authorization could support a separately registered bounded 2019 race acquisition if a defensible transfer question remains. Before that, require an explicit mapping from observed historical states to a usable future pre-weekend input, preserve unknown state as unknown, and specify sample comparability and independent repeatability before response/outcome inspection. Qualifying outlaps versus hotlaps are not automatically comparable race-trim controls.

**Verification/artifact:** `results/e202_drs_source_audit.json` records counts, parser version/hash and scope. An initial AST verifier incorrectly required only one assignment to the variable `drs` and failed on the later array-conversion assignment; corrected to identify the channel lookup specifically, with no source/result change. Independent `Path.rglob` and `os.walk` inventories agree on the complete filename set; assertions would fail on any race file, a different total, or missing parser fallback/comment. Source trace from `f1/telemetry.py` and `f1/energy.py` independently confirms the existing closed-state mask. This is a source/code/documentation audit; no model/test implementation, new data requests, source authentication, labels, winner/podium scoring, production changes, or new 2026 evaluation. Standard feature leak checks are not a substitute for state observability and are not rerun for documentation/artifact-only changes. No inner/outer numbers arise; frozen winner comparisons remain E179 exploratory34/70 versus race-points/ens32/70. No paired bootstrap/null is applicable without a new model score.

**Next constraint:** choose a genuinely different observable mechanism, or first resolve the deployment/source question above. Historical within-race wind response is a possible source-design audit (distinct from E16 event-level weather), but it needs the same nuisance-support and strict-before-weekend checks; no wind response, gate, source expansion or forecast model has been registered or run. Research goal remains active and unmet at75% exact winners across all races; strict overlap remains secondary.

### E203 — Historical wind-magnitude design support (registered before measurement)

**Question/novelty:** E16 tested event-level weather inputs (including hindsight weather), not historical car-specific within-race wind response. E198's thermal design failed and stays closed. Candidate A is a measurable wind-magnitude response after controlling race/stint evolution; candidate B is wind variation explained by those nuisances, leaving inadequate independent support. Choose a design-only screen using the already recovered E197 pilot, not another model/parameter search. Scalar wind speed is distinct from track-temperature sensitivity but cannot identify headwind/crosswind effects; direction-specific geometry and gust measurements are excluded from this fixed test. A single weather station may not describe the whole circuit.

**Contract/source:** fixed2019 Spain(round5), Hungary12, Italy14 only. Join E197's already saved minute-weather `WindSpeed` to its aligned dry lap rows on exact season/round/weather_time, many-to-one with duplicate source keys rejected. No network, account, current/future event source, outcome fit or new2026 inspection. FastF13.9.0 installed `_api.weather_data` explicitly gives WindSpeed in **m/s**, with minute updates; official library docs consulted via Context7. Preserve E198's eligible-row definition, normalized event-time phase and shared alternating-distinct-weather-reading halves **before** excluding missing/nonfinite/negative wind. This preserves the fixed split rather than selecting it for wind values. Missing wind rows excluded explicitly, not filled with calm wind. Original driver/event combinations remain in diagnostics even with zero eligible wind rows. Empty/non-pilot event universe and duplicate driver/lap keys reject through the existing prepare contract.

**Fixed nuisance design:** reuse E198 intercept, cubic normalized time, stint intercepts(first omitted), compound-specific tyre-age/10 linear and quadratic, Position/20 and close indicator. Add measured TrackTemp/10 as one linear nuisance, to keep a simple temperature association from being mistaken for wind support. No lap duration, position outcome, winner label or response coefficient is fitted. For each driver/event/full/half subset, project wind onto this design using least squares rcond1e−10; save residuals, rank, degrees of freedom, RMS and fraction of raw wind variance. This diagnoses available variation, not causal identifiability or effective independent sample size.

**Gate/tripwire:** each full and both shared-block halves require ≥15rows, ≥10distinct readings, ≥10residual degrees of freedom, residual wind RMS≥**0.5m/s**, and residual/raw centered variance≥**0.10** (constant wind→fraction0). Require ≥10drivers passing **all three subsets** in at least **two of the three fixed races**. These are predeclared pilot-screen thresholds, not physical effect/sensor bounds. Failure closes this magnitude/controls/split pilot without threshold relaxation, removing controls, choosing a supported race alone, switching to direction/squared speed or expanding years to force support. Passing permits only a separately registered response-repeatability/temporal-transfer study, not a winner model or forecast gain. Deployment would require past-only sensitivity and a permissible pre-weekend forecast or historical robustness feature; actual target-weekend wind remains forbidden.

**Verification before scoring:** synthetic nuisance-confounded wind, known orthogonal variation, thermal-only confounding, constant/empty/one-row data; missing/negative wind, exact weather joins, duplicate/non-pilot rejection and response-label mutation invariance pass. Existing functions read before reuse; no shared function edited, no dependency or production change. Extend independent saved-artifact audit with separately built columns/SVD projection, weather merge/splits, eligibility, every diagnostic/residual, gate and response-label mutation checks. Standard leak/pre-weekend/profile/guard/metric tests required. Commit/push setup before real-source measurement, results separately. No winner/podium selector is made, so inner/outer accuracy, paired winner bootstrap and winner-label null do not arise; frozen baselines unchanged. Goal remains75% exact pre-weekend winners across all races.

**Result (E203): fixed wind-magnitude support gate failed.** All **3,151** source lap rows have eligible finite nonnegative wind, with no exclusions;60driver/event combinations and180 full/half diagnostics. Drivers passing **all three subsets: Spain0/20, Hungary0/20, Italy0/20**. Passing individual full/half0/half1 subset counts: Spain **4/0/3**, Hungary **0/0/0**, Italy **10/0/0**. None reaches the required two supported events.

Median residual wind RMS full/half0/half1(m/s): Spain **0.4744/0.3644/0.3992**, Hungary **0.3691/0.3160/0.3426**, Italy **0.5027/0.3649/0.3498**. Median residual fractions of raw wind variance: Spain **0.6485/0.5040/0.4128**, Hungary **0.6276/0.4900/0.5559**, Italy **0.5620/0.3378/0.2688**. A meaningful fraction remains, but no half0 subset in any event has ≥0.5m/s residual RMS. Some short Italy halves additionally fail row/degree-of-freedom screens. The failed amplitude screen is a predeclared measurement criterion, not proof of absent aerodynamic wind sensitivity.

**Verdict:** no response coefficient, broad acquisition, new ranking feature, winner model, outer score or2026 evaluation. Do not lower the threshold, fit only Italy's full rows, weaken controls, switch to wind-direction/squared-speed variants or expand the source merely to force this pilot through. No accuracy improvement is claimed; winner target75% remains active/unmet, and frozen all-race references unchanged. The historical weather source remains valid independently of this measurement failure.

**Verification:** setup **f275ed0** was committed/pushed before real-source measurement. Synthetic tests and all five standard no-leak/pre-weekend/profile/guard/metric checks pass. Independent artifact audit reconstructs the wind join via a keyed lookup instead of merge, event phase/shared halves and every nuisance column, then uses orthonormal SVD rather than coefficient fitting. All180 ranks/degrees/diagnostics,6,302 residual rows, orthogonality, gates and response-label mutations reproduce. One exploratory console `groupby(...).support.sum()` summary returned zero counts inconsistently with its own saved True rows; the reported individual-subset counts above were checked by direct per-group boolean sums and independent rowwise support reconstruction, not that console aggregate. Driver/event full-and-both-halves gate is separately reconstructed and unchanged. Main tracked files unchanged; no credential/network/source writes. Saved `results/e203_*`.

**Next constraint:** new experiments need a distinct information mechanism and a defensible future pre-weekend input, beyond the now-failed scalar wind/temperature and traffic-response pilots. A source audit is useful for rejecting a false avenue but does not move winner accuracy by itself. Keep the objective at75% exact winners, not successful source acquisition or passing an easier metric.

### E204 — First-full-lap post-stop response (registered before extraction)

**Question/decision:** can prior team tyre warm-up behavior improve future winning-team forecasts? E121 measured a collapsed costly pit-lane tail; E144 required a nearby opponent and measured undercut position reversal. Neither measures first-full-lap performance after an ordinary stop. Candidate A is a repeatable team/car warm-up response; candidate B is local traffic/management/tyre-state noise. This measurement distinguishes them before any winner model. Known: all21 2019 race lap caches available in earlier audits. Assumed: enough comparable stop sequences and a portable team component. Needed: teammate agreement and strictly later-event forecast improvement over zero/global and shuffled team identity. No expensive source acquisition.

**Fixed source/contract:** cached2019 races1–21 only, HTTP disabled through existing cached_session; official existing result metadata supplies driver-code/team mapping, never outcome labels. Raw lap keys must be unique. A stop requires pit-in on the preceding driver lap, pit-out on the following lap and10<pit-lane seconds<60, excluding most red-flag/drive-through outliers without proving an ordinary service. Take the **first full lap after the pit-out lap** and its next3laps, exact consecutive numbers. All4 must pass the existing E190 green/accurate/non-pit/>lap1/<107%-driver-median cleaner, match the outlap stint/compound, be on SOFT/MEDIUM/HARD, have increasing tyre age by1 and one nonmissing position. No fresh-tyre requirement or observed track-temperature control; selection/DRS/traffic, used tyres and nonlinear evolution remain confounders. Empty/missing blocks excluded, not zero-imputed. No target-weekend input, new2026 feed/evaluation, outside text, market or authentication.

**Response fixed before counts:** regress the following3full-lap durations on offsets1,2,3 and extrapolate to offset0. First-full-lap excess over that extrapolation, divided by the4-lap mean and multiplied100, is the percent-time response. Algebraic weights **[1,−4/3,−1/3,+2/3]** cancel constant and linear trends; negative response is allowed. This is not immediate outlap performance or identified tyre physics. Within each driver/event take median over eligible stops. Team full response is equal mean of the two listed drivers, only when both measured. Lexically first/second listed driver yields disjoint teammate halves; a driver change may change these labels. Center each response/full/half across an event only when≥6teams are measurable for that quantity; halves are assessed on their own marginal coverage then intersected. Retain every listed team/event, including missing.

**Coverage/measurement gates:** ≥12events with≥6full teams, ≥60joint centered teammate-half rows, and ≥60full centered temporal rows with≥3earlier measured team events. Only if all counts pass compute performance. Reuse the existing fixed10-event-half-life,5-zero-observation shrinkage prior (E191), trained on strictly earlier2019events. Zero centered response is the baseline; matched null shuffles team responses within completed events with seed204 then forecasts with the same rule. Require teammate correlation≥.30 with race-cluster bootstrap lower95%>0, temporal correlation≥.15, positive MSE gain over zero with lower95%>0, and positive MSE gain over matched null with lower95%>0. Existing cluster_interval uses4000race resamples, seed191; no seed or scale search. Passing permits a separately registered development-period feature test, never75% success. Failure closes this source/contrast/aggregation/forecast formulation without threshold/window/compound/normalization tuning or broader acquisition to force a pass.

**Verification:** read E190/E191 helpers and their callers before reuse; shared modules unchanged. Synthetic isolated first-lap penalty and linear-trend cancellation, pit/caution/inaccurate/stint/position/age/missing/duplicate block rejection, outcome-column invariance and strict-prior forecast timing pass. A synthetic fixture initially inferred datetime NaT rather than timedelta NaT; corrected fixture dtype before extraction. An editing duplicate was removed before first successful import/test; no outcome or source observation involved. Current official FastF1 timing/method docs and installed schema were consulted in prior source audits; this uses those existing lap columns, no new API. Commit/push setup before extraction; results separately. No winner selector is scored: inner/outer accuracy and paired winner bootstrap/winner-label null do not arise. User objective remains75% exact pre-weekend winners across all races; strict podium overlap secondary.

**Implemented specification (frozen in setup df81778):** actual pit-in on the preceding lap and pit-out must be10–60seconds apart, strictly inside both limits, excluding unpaired race starts/long stoppages. Select the first **full lap after the pit-out lap** and its next three laps. All four must satisfy E190 green/accurate/non-pit/time-known/>lap1/<107%-of-driver-median clean selection; be consecutive, same dry SOFT/MEDIUM/HARD compound and stint as the pit-out row, increasing tyre age by1, and finite unchanged position. The raw-second contrast is `[1,−4/3,−1/3,+2/3]·lap_times / mean(lap_times) ×100`: first full lap minus a line fitted to the next three and extrapolated backward. Constant and linear time trends cancel in the numerator; nonlinear fuel/tyre evolution, traffic, DRS, management and warm-up remain mixed. No causal tyre or crew interpretation. Each driver's median across qualifying stop blocks is used; alphabetical entrant codes define independent teammate halves. A full team/event response is the equal mean only when both entrant-driver medians exist. Full/each-half values are centered separately only with≥6measured teams, with no requirement that the half support sets coincide.

Independently centered half0/half1 marginal coverage **69/49** team/event rows; their intersection **22**, versus60required. Chronological opportunities with≥3earlier valid centered measurements and a measured current label **0**, versus60required. Saved summaries therefore contain no split/temporal correlation, bootstrap interval, MSE comparison or matched-null performance; those steps were not run after source coverage failed. Do not infer that failure to run a null means observed superiority.

### E205 — Earlier-race isolated lap disruptions for exact winners (registered before extraction/scoring)

**Question/choice:** E101/E167 used historical cause rates; E120 used short-running exposure. Neither the full-log search nor the inspected implementations tests isolated slow laps embedded inside completed green stints. Mechanical causes are missing in the later official statuses, and outside text is excluded; do not assign mechanical labels or use the Wikipedia cause asset. Candidate A is a team-level disruption burden carrying additional winner information; candidate B is local traffic/driver-error/measurement noise with no forecast benefit. Test A directly against exact-winner labels, rather than spending another experiment on a correlation-only claim. This is observational and does not distinguish mechanical warnings, traffic, driver mistakes or management.

**Fixed completed-race source:** cached2018–22 FastF1 race laps only,103scheduled events. Network disabled and HTTP blocked via existing cached_session. Report every missing session (2018R14 is historically absent; no special fallback). A valid center lap and its immediately preceding/following driver laps must each be green TrackStatus1, accurate, after lap1, nonpit, positive finite lap duration; all three have the same known stint and dry SOFT/MEDIUM/HARD compound, increasing tyre age by1, and consecutive lap numbers. No slow-lap clipping: those laps are the candidate signal. Center is disrupted iff seconds≥**1.02×mean(neighbor seconds)**. Count eligible center windows and disrupted windows for every driver present, including zero-eligible drivers. Windows can overlap, so counts are exposure measures, not independent trials; no causal/event-count significance is inferred. This difference removes a linear local trend but can miss sustained slowing and includes traffic artifacts. No source outcome, final position, current grid, telemetry or mechanical-cause status is used. Source coverage requires≥95available events and≥80%available driver/event rows with≥3eligible centers before model fit; otherwise stop without changing eligibility.

**One feature, frozen model:** attach source to existing driver/team/event metadata; duplicates or unmatched source keys reject. For each target, only source events with k<targetk enter. Weight counts by10-event half-life. Past-global rate=(weighted disruptions+.5)/(weighted eligible+1); team rate=(weighted team disruptions+100×past-global)/(weighted team eligible+100). Missing team history falls back to that past-global; no source history→.5. This is empirical shrinkage, not a calibrated failure probability. Raw constructor aliases retained. Add only `prior_team_disruption` to E168's fixed logistic winner model (C=.03,100-event training half-life, train2019+, history2018). ### E206 — Rolling-restart timing/cohort source pilot (registered before extraction)

**Question/novelty:** initial lap-one gains/laps-led, event safety-car frequency, E144 undercuts and E204 post-pit warm-up do not measure rolling-restart order changes among non-pitting cars. Candidate A is a usable restart-execution source distinct from initial starts; candidate B is timing staleness, pit/retirement rank inflation or insufficient clean windows. This is a source test before assuming a persistent driver skill. Choose fixed2019 Spain(round5), Britain10 and Brazil20: known historical SC-event settings, not selected from any winner-model outcome. No source expansion if this fixed pilot fails its gate.

**Inputs/contract:** existing cached historical race laps, track_status timeline and parsed `_extended_timing_data` stream, whose schema was audited in E199. Official FastF1 installed track-status docs define4=SC and1=clear;6/7are VSC,5red. Current official source/timing docs consulted via Context7. All source network calls blocked and offline cache required, no authentication/new source acquisition or2026data. Map numeric driver IDs through the same completed race's lap metadata; unknown identities and duplicate driver/lap or timeline-time keys reject. Parsed stream duplicate driver/time keeps last, then stable chronological sort. No pace/winner/finish label, raw-car telemetry, gap-value clock, document or market input.

**Fixed definition:** candidate green time t is an immediate status4→1transition (not VSC/standing starts, not4→yellow→green). Before snapshot=t−5seconds; after=t+120seconds. Latest status at before must be4, and any status transition in(t,after] must remain1. For each driver, take last stream row ≤each snapshot, require row age0..30seconds inclusive and finite integer positions1..30. This is **stream-row freshness**, not independently verified fresh Position observations: FastF1 forward-fills Position, and latency remains a limitation. Exclude any driver with a pit-in/out flag on a lap overlapping[before,after], using completed lap boundaries. Require at least one accurate wholly green lap starting≥t and completing≤after, plus a recorded driver lap completion≥after (evidence of continued running). Cars failing these checks do not join the cohort; lapped traffic and imperfect session-time boundaries can remain confounders.

Both snapshots must have unique position values within the remaining cohort, which must contain≥6cars; otherwise reject the whole restart. Re-rank **the same eligible cohort** at before/after; gain=before_rank−after_rank and gains sum0. Thus a car that pits/retires outside the cohort cannot automatically generate a measured gain for the rest. This is observational relative order over a fixed120seconds, not a pure launch/driver/causal passing metric. Green transitions lacking a full120-second clear window or a full green lap are excluded explicitly; do not move snapshots or relax exclusions after counting.

**Gate:** at least one usable restart in at least2of3fixed sessions, ≥3usable restart windows overall and ≥30driver/restart rows. Passing permits only separately registered full-inner-period acquisition/measurement/forecast evaluation, not a model promotion or accuracy claim. Failure closes this exact source/window/cohort pilot without changing ages, duration, status logic, participants or race selection. No winner selector here, so no new inner/outer accuracy, paired winner bootstrap or winner-label null; standings/E168/ens results remain unchanged.

**Verification:** synthetic known pair reversal with all other ranks preserved, removal/re-ranking of a pitting leader without false gains, continued-running exclusion, VSC/caution-window rejection, exact30-second versus31-second age boundary, duplicate-position/lap rejection, empty cohort and post-window stream mutation invariance pass. Read E199/E190 helpers and call paths before reuse; shared source/feature modules unchanged. Commit/push setup before real-source extraction; independently reconstruct all pilot transitions, checkpoints, source samples, eligible participants/ranks and gate with separate binary-search selection. Standard guards required alongside new source checks. No target-weekend feature is added; source timing only permits strictly earlier completed races in any subsequent feature. User objective remains75% exact pre-weekend winners across all races, strict overlap secondary, active/unmet.

**Result (E206): source gate passed.** Four immediate SC→green transitions across the fixed pilot: Spain1, Britain1, Brazil2. The first window in each event is usable, with **16/15/16participants**, **47driver/restart observations** and **three usable restarts across all three races**. Brazil's second candidate fails the uninterrupted120-second-clear-window rule and is excluded, with zero retained participants. Both position snapshots are unique in every usable window.

Observed cohort-relative gain distribution: **−4:1, −2:2, −1:6, 0:27, +1:9, +2:1, +3:1**. Each restart's gains sum exactly0;20/47observations are nonzero. This verifies a nonconstant order-change source, not driver-skill persistence, causal overtaking attribution or incremental winner information. Three dependent windows are not a forecast evaluation. No inner/outer winner accuracy, probability model, paired winner bootstrap or null score was produced; all frozen references unchanged.

### E208 — Same-compound Q1 ideal-lap history versus matched actual laps (registered before extraction/scoring)

**Question/novelty audit:** f1.sessions/f1.profile already measured best sectors across the **whole Q session**, and their broad block failed E9/E11; E40 tested ordinary Q1-gap history and E188 included Q1 gaps in fusion. This is not a new data feed or permission to tune those failed formulations. The inspected log/code contains no physical sum of best sectors **within one dry compound and Q1 only**, tested against an otherwise identical actual-lap history for exact winners. Candidate A: sector recombination preserves underlying car capability hidden by incomplete lap execution; candidate B: it adds noise/programme/track-evolution variation and no winner information. ### E209 — Stationary pit-interval raw-car source pilot (registered before acquisition)

**Novelty/decision:** E9/E11 pit summaries and E121 costly-stop tails use total pit-lane timestamps, mixing travel, service, traffic and waiting. E204 measures post-stop warm-up, not stationary time. A raw Speed=0 interval inside pit timestamps is a distinct measurement candidate. Candidate A is consistently measurable near-stationary service/waiting time; candidate B is missing telemetry, timestamp error or multiple pauses masquerading as a crew signal. Even a source pass would not establish pit-crew skill or forecastability.

**Source audit/padding trap:** installed FastF1 3.9.0 `_api.car_data` expands incomplete drivers to the most-complete Date series, fills missing numeric channels with0, and leaves those added rows' original Time asNaT. A Speed=0 test alone can therefore invent a stop. Use original API frames **before Session.load_telemetry reconstructs Time**, retain that missingness and make Time=NaT rows break zero runs. Current official FastF1 raw/time docs and installed `_calculate_t0_date` inspected: calibrate car-only origin=max(Date−rawTime) across all cars, rounded milliseconds; compute sample session time from Date−origin. This copies the documented/library calibration assumption, not a separately verified absolute time reference. Race parsed-car cache was absent in E202; no2019race raw-car payload has yet been inspected for this pilot.

**Fixed bounded acquisition:** existing E197-authorized F1 account and `scripts_run_ff1.sh` helper only, reading its two keys at runtime without displaying them. No new account, purchase or licence. Pre-auth with existing password method, report authentication errors by exception type only. Fixed E190/E197pilot:2019Spain5/Hungary12/Italy14. Load cached race laps offline/HTTP blocked; during raw car acquisition allow only each registered session's **CarData.z.jsonStream** primary and built-in mirror URLs. Fetch no position/other feed, outside document/market, target-weekend or2026source. Existing parsed/HTTP cache may satisfy a request; report request statuses and cache availability without treating cache use as a new unauthenticated-access result. No parameter/source/URL expansion on failure; partial per-event metadata checkpoint saved.

**Frozen measurement:** pair each driver's recorded pit-in with the following lap's pit-out, strictly10–60seconds apart, exclude red-status5 on either lap. Every such window remains in denominator, even missing car records. Deduplicate raw Date last-write, chronological sort, retain only Date within the closed pit interval. Zero sample iff **Speed==0 and original Time nonmissing**. Split runs on nonzero/missing-original-Time samples or Date gaps>0.5seconds. A run needs observed positive-speed immediate neighbors on both sides, each≤0.5seconds from its zero endpoint. Save zero-span `lower`=lastzero−firstzero, moving-bracket `upper`=nextmoving−previousmoving, midpoint average; require1≤zero-span≤10seconds and bracket width upper−lower≤0.75seconds. Exactly one qualifying run makes a usable window; zero/multiple runs fail. These are sampling brackets conditional on approximately continuous stopping between samples, **not verified physical bounds or wheel-work durations**. Queueing, release wait, damage repair, sensor quantization, clock calibration and feed loss remain confounders; no causal crew label.

**Source gate:** at least2of3fixed events each have≥15normal pit windows and≥70%usable windows; ≥30usable windows total. Passing only permits separately registered measurement repeatability/temporal forecast work, not winner feature or75%claim. Failure closes this source/zero/run/bracket/pilot rule without changing cadence, thresholds, statuses, missingness handling or race selection. Do not infer costly-stop-tail predictive value from a normal-stop interval gate. No winner selector/scoring, paired winner bootstrap or outcome-label null in this source-only study.

**Verification/contract:** raw Date/Time/Speed frames and cached pit-lap metadata in, per-normal-stop provenance/coverage/brackets out; malformed offsets fail rather than silently impute observed zeros. Synthetic known span/brackets, all-padding false stop, padding inside a run, multiple true runs, missing moving bracket, empty window, post-window speed independence and non2019URL rejection pass. Existing source/request/helper paths read; shared modules unchanged. Standard five guards required; independent raw-cache scalar Date/Time origin, pit matching, run segmentation, brackets and complete gate reconstruction after acquisition. No target feature is created; future usage must include strictly earlier completed events only. Commit/push setup before auth/feed calls; result separately. User objective75%exact pre-weekend winners across all races remains active/unmet; strict overlap secondary.

**Result (E209): stationary-interval source gate passed.** All three registered raw-car feeds return primaryHTTP200 through the existing authorized account/helper:2019Spain5 **648,560samples**, Hungary12 **655,260**, Italy14 **549,020**, total **1,852,840**. All parsed frames in this pilot have **zero Time-missing padded rows**. The known parser zero-fill risk is defended by synthetic tests; it is not observed contamination in these three feeds. No account created, payment/licence acceptance or outside-document model input. Requests after pre-auth are restricted to the fixed CarData endpoints; source provenance retained.

Normal pit windows **37/24/28**; usable single bounded zero-speed intervals **28/21/20** = **75.68%/87.50%/71.43%**, totaling **69/89**. Each event individually passes≥15windows/≥70%coverage, so the required two events and30usable total pass. Median lower/upper/midpoint spans(seconds): Spain **2.6801/3.1600/2.9201**, Hungary **2.5996/3.0796/2.8396**, Italy **2.7196/3.1800/2.9498**. Across usable windows, median bound width **0.479932seconds**, maximum **0.599915**, below registered0.75. These bracket values concern the recorded speed samples under the fixed source-clock approximation, not certified bounds on real wheel-service duration.

**Verdict:** distinct observable stationary-duration source is feasible; no crew-effect persistence or forecast value yet established. No new winner/podium selector, accuracy, paired winner bootstrap or outcome null. The75%exact pre-weekend winner goal stays active/unmet, frozen all-race results unchanged. ### E211 — Many-lap fixed-speed braking signature (registered before source measurement)

**Decision/novelty:** audit of E9/E97/E202 shows few-qualifying-lap acceleration/engine signatures failed and a DRS open/closed experiment still lacks a reliable current-state counterpart. Do not reopen them. E9's reference-corner deceleration used a fastest qualifying lap; no inspected test uses many completed-race laps with a fixed250→150km/h brake-on transit and lap-balanced aggregation. Candidate A is a measurable braking tendency with incremental future pace information; candidate B is driver/corner/traffic/fuel mixture or sampling noise. Choose the DRS-independent braking source rather than infer unknown wing states. This is not a pure brake-force/grip/driver-skill label: Brake is boolean, tyres/fuel/traffic and corner mixture remain confounders.

**Scope/source:** all21cached2019races and raw CarData already acquired in E210; offline/HTTP forbidden, no account/auth/new feed/year, position geometry, raw2026or outside document/market. Reuse E190 green/accurate/nonpit/>lap1/<107%-driver-median lap selection unchanged; it is retrospective completed-event cleaning, not a live predictor. Raw Date deduplicate-last then sorted; preserve original Time.notna to exclude zero-filled missing samples. Car-only origin=max(Date−rawTime), millisecond rounding per E209/E210. Use Date-derived elapsed seconds and integer-nanosecond source gaps, no derivative smoothing or resampled curve. Brake/Speed fields persist without requiring DRS state; future regime predictive transfer still unverified.

**Fixed segment:** upper crossing consecutive samples Speed_i≥250 and Speed_i+1<250, with originalTime observed and Brake=True. Continue while each sample is observed/brake-on, strictly positive Date gap≤0.5s and Speed nonincreasing. Stop at first sample≤150; require≥4raw samples inclusive. Linearly interpolate250and150crossing times between bracketing speeds. Accept duration0.4..3seconds inclusive; save raw first/last sample times, crossings, duration and sample count. Consumed accepted segments cannot overlap. Reject missing/false-brake/gap/nonmonotonic/incomplete runs; no DRS, RPM, position or target outcome used. Both complete bracketing endpoints must lie in one clean driver's half-open lap interval[lapstart,lapend), preventing sample sharing across lap halves.

**Aggregation:** median segment duration per driver/lap, then mean lap medians per driver/event, requiring≥10measurable laps; no weighting by number of corners per lap. Disjoint halves use actual LapNumber parity, each driver/half requiring≥5measurable laps. Team full/each half=equal mean of both listed drivers, only if both meet that quantity's threshold; retain all210team/event rows including missing. Independently field-center full/half columns only with≥6measured teams for that column. Source keys unique; unknown source-driver/event metadata and nonfinite duration reject. No track/compound/driver-subset selection or tuning.

**Signature gate:** reuse E204.evaluate/E191past_response unchanged:10-event half-life,5zero-prior event equivalents, strictly earlier labeled team races; baseline0relative signature, matched within-event label permutation seed204,4000race-cluster bootstrap resamples seed191. Need≥12races with≥6full teams,≥60paired half rows,≥60temporal rows with≥3earlier valid team measurements before performance scoring. Then half-correlation≥.30(lower95%>0), temporal correlation≥.15, positive MSE gain vszero and vsnull, both lower95%>0. All required; these generic thresholds were fixed before this new source was measured.

**Additional pace-utility gate:** stable source identity alone is insufficient (E97). Use only the **2019**rows of frozen `results/e194_forecasts_errors_scales.csv`: its strictly pre-event mean forecast and later actual relative pace define already-audited error labels. Equal-driver team mean errors, field-centered across≥6valid teams per event, are future targets only. Join prior braking forecast, require≥3earlier signature measurements, ≥60rows across≥12events. Only if signature count coverage and these utility counts pass, compute prediction vs later pace-error correlation, expected positive(longer braking/slower-than-predicted pace); require≥.15with lower95%>0 using the fixed race bootstrap. Full gate=signature gate AND utility gate. Utility performance may be reported when signature quality fails but cannot rescue it. No target braking observation or pace error enters its own predictor.

**Verification/protocol:** existing raw source/parser/time APIs already documented/audited; source/ranking paths read before reuse, shared modules unchanged. Synthetic interpolated crossings, too-short/padded/false-brake/gappy/nonmonotone/empty runs, arbitrary DRS changes, equal-lap weighting, half/full minimum counts and non2019source rejection pass. Require independent all21raw source/clean-lap/segment reconstruction, driver-lap medians/team centers, scalar past forecasts, fixed null/bootstrap/gate, frozen pace-error label joins and raw-source target/future timing invariance. Standard five guards required. No winner selector or new winner/podium accuracy; paired winner bootstrap/winner-label null not applicable here. Passing permits separately registered inner winner-feature work, not75%success; failure closes this precise source/band/filter/aggregation/forecast/utility protocol without threshold/entity/half/year/model tuning.
